- **spiritual-minded** (`spiritual_minded#1`, cut-off 3) by [religious](../../../traits/instructions/religious.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve a spiritual orientation toward life, but spiritual-minded centers on meaning and the sacred in a possibly non-institutional way, while religious specifically draws on faith, scripture and tradition as authority; each can exist without the other."), Opus 3 ("Both describe seeing life through a sacred or spiritual frame; religious narrows this to faith grounded in scripture, doctrine and tradition, so it differs mainly in scope.")
  Gloss: This means viewing life through a religious or spiritual lens, treating questions of meaning, purpose and the sacred as central to how one understands the world and oneself.
- **square** (`square#1`, cut-off 3) by [traditional](../../../traits/instructions/traditional.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve preferring the conventional and established over the new, but 'square' adds a social dimension of disregarding hipness and fashion while 'traditional' emphasizes preferring proven methods and wisdom."), Opus 3 ("Both centre on preferring the established and conventional over the new. Square narrows this to social tastes and manners and adds indifference to being hip, while traditional covers methods and practices broadly.")
  Gloss: This means keeping to conventional tastes and manners, preferring what is traditional and familiar over what is fashionable, and showing no interest in being seen as hip or current.
- **stellar** (`stellar#1`, cut-off 3) by [competent](../../../traits/instructions/competent.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both concern work quality, but stellar means outstanding, excellent performance while competent means reliably adequate, capable work; this is the same quality at different degrees."), Opus 3 ("Both describe quality of work performance, but stellar is outstanding excellence while competent is solid adequacy, so stellar is the same concept carried much further.")
  Gloss: This means consistently delivering outstanding work, performing at an excellent level in whatever task one takes on, and raising the quality of results across the board.
- **storied** (`storied#1`, cut-off 3) by [famous](../../../traits/instructions/famous.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both concern renown, but 'storied' stresses a legendary, tale-laden reputation while 'famous' stresses being widely recognized by strangers and living in public; neither implies the other."), Opus 3 ("Storied is fame carried further into legendary reputation, the same concept as being widely known, only intensified and given a legendary tinge.")
  Gloss: This means having a famous, legendary reputation.
- **strenuous** (`strenuous#1`, cut-off 3) by [industrious](../../../traits/instructions/industrious.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve working hard, but strenuous stresses intense vigor and effort in each task, while industrious stresses habitual diligence, persistence and dislike of idleness, so each adds something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Both describe working hard as a matter of course: strenuous stresses the vigor and intensity of the effort, industrious its steady, habitual diligence.")
  Gloss: This means throwing great effort and vigor into every task, pushing hard and energetically at one's work until it is done, and counting exertion as the proper way to go about things.
- **sublime** (`sublime#1`, cut-off 3) by [dignified](../../../traits/instructions/dignified.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve a dignified, poised bearing, but sublime stresses lofty, elevated excellence in every gesture while dignified stresses self-respect, composure and avoiding ridicule, so each adds something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Sublime is dignified bearing carried further, to a lofty excellence that seems raised above the ordinary, so they differ mainly in degree.")
  Gloss: This means carrying oneself with a lofty, dignified excellence, so that one's manner and bearing seem elevated above the ordinary in every gesture and word.
- **sucker** (`sucker#1`, cut-off 3) by [credulous](../../../traits/instructions/credulous.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve readily believing what one is told, but 'sucker' adds being repeatedly exploited without learning, while 'credulous' adds a general disposition to accept claims without evidence, so each has something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Both are readily believing what one is told; sucker stresses the consequence of being repeatedly conned, which is the same gullibility taken to its exploited outcome.")
  Gloss: This means believing what one is told, trusting strangers' promises, and being repeatedly fooled or taken advantage of without learning to see the con coming.
- **susceptible** (`susceptible#1`, cut-off 4) by [malleable](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate): Sonnet 3 ("Both describe easily changing opinions under others' pressure, with malleable adding plans and loyalties but essentially the same concept."), Opus 4 ("Both describe readily yielding opinions to whoever pushes hardest; malleable extends slightly to plans and loyalties but is essentially the same concept.")
  Gloss: This means being easily swayed by others' arguments and pressure, so that one's opinions shift toward whoever pushed hardest or spoke last.
- **swift** (`swift#1`, cut-off 3) by [hurried](../../../traits/instructions/hurried.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve acting immediately without delay, but 'swift' is prompt, effective responsiveness while 'hurried' adds a frantic, indiscriminate sense of urgency about everything; they share a core yet each adds something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Both describe dealing with things at once without delay; hurried pushes this further into treating everything as urgent and rushing regardless of inconvenience.")
  Gloss: This means acting the moment a need appears, answering messages and requests without delay, and moving from decision to action with no pause for second thoughts.
- **tart** (`tart#1`, cut-off 3) by [harsh](../../../traits/instructions/harsh.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve cutting, wounding speech, but harsh is broader (severity, quick judgment, unsoftened truths) while tart is specifically a caustic, sharp-tongued style of remark."), Opus 3 ("Both centre on cutting, wounding remarks, but harsh is broader, adding general severity and quick judgment beyond tart's sharp verbal edge.")
  Gloss: This means speaking in a sharp, cutting way, with a caustic edge to one's remarks that wounds more than it teases.
- **theistic** (`theistic#1`, cut-off 3) by [Muslim](../../../traits/instructions/muslim.json): Sonnet 2 ("Being Muslim entails theism (belief in one God), but theistic is a general belief in god(s) with no specific religion, while Muslim adds specific faith and practices; a theist need not be Muslim, and Muslim is a subset case."), Opus 3 ("Being Muslim is a specific form of theism that adds particular doctrines and practices, so it implies theism but is much narrower in scope.")
  Gloss: This means holding that a god or gods exist and that belief in them is a settled part of how one understands the world and one's place in it.
- **thrilling** (`thrilling#1`, cut-off 3) by [animated](../../../traits/instructions/animated.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe lively, enthusiastic, vivid communication, but thrilling stresses energizing and mobilizing others while animated stresses expressive imagery and style, so they share a core with different emphases."), Opus 3 ("Both describe energetic, vivid, enthusiastic communication, but thrilling stresses energizing and mobilizing others while animated stresses lively expressive language and imagery.")
  Gloss: This means bringing a lively, electric energy into every exchange, speaking with vivid momentum and enthusiasm that wakes up the people nearby and gets them moving.
- **timeless** (`timeless#1`, cut-off 3) by [steady](../../../traits/instructions/steady.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve constancy of character, but 'timeless' is about staying the same and never seeming dated across years and fashions, while 'steady' is about consistency of tone and view within a single conversation, so they overlap without either implying the other."), Opus 3 ("Both describe holding the same values, manner and view without swerving, differing mainly in scope: across years and fashions versus within a single conversation.")
  Gloss: This means keeping one's character steady across years and fashions, holding the same values and manner whatever changes around one, so that nothing about one's outlook or conduct ever seems outdated.
- **trifling** (`trifling#1`, cut-off 3) by [lighthearted](../../../traits/instructions/lighthearted.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve a light, joking treatment of serious matters, but trifling adds carelessness and disinterest in weighty subjects, while lighthearted is a breezy, easy disposition without implying dismissiveness."), Opus 3 ("Both describe treating even weighty matters with easy, joking lightness; trifling is the same quality carried further, into careless dismissiveness and disinterest in being serious.")
  Gloss: This means treating matters with a light, careless air, joking off serious questions and showing little interest in weighty subjects or in being taken seriously.
- **ubiquitous** (`ubiquitous#1`, cut-off 3) by [frequent poster](../../../traits/instructions/frequent_poster.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve being present everywhere, but 'frequent poster' is specifically about heavy online posting and engagement, while 'ubiquitous' is general presence in all settings; they overlap without either implying the other."), Opus 3 ("Both describe showing up everywhere, but frequent poster is limited to online activity, a narrower case of the general omnipresence in ubiquitous.")
  Gloss: This means appearing in every setting and context one enters, never confined to a single place or occasion, turning up wherever the talk, work, or daily life happens to go.
- **unbecoming** (`unbecoming#1`, cut-off 3) by [unladylike](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe habitually improper conduct, but 'unladylike' is a narrower case, focused on coarse, boisterous behaviour that breaks genteel expectations for women, while 'unbecoming' covers any undignified conduct."), Opus 3 ("Both describe improper, undignified conduct, but unladylike is a narrower, gendered form centred on coarse boisterousness flouting genteel norms for women.")
  Gloss: This means acting improperly and undignifiedly as a matter of course, conducting oneself in ways that lower the standing of the occasion and of oneself.
- **unflattering** (`unflattering#1`, cut-off 3) by [harsh](../../../traits/instructions/harsh.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve blunt, critical judgments delivered without softening, but 'harsh' adds severity, heavy-handedness and cutting replies, while 'unflattering' emphasizes withholding praise; each has features the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Both describe blunt, critical, unsoftened judgment of people; harsh is a broader, more severe version that adds heavy-handedness and cutting retorts.")
  Gloss: This means speaking bluntly and critically about things and people, holding back praise and offering harsh judgments even when kindness would be easy.
- **unseemly** (`unseemly#1`, cut-off 3) by [rude](../../../traits/instructions/rude.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve bad manners in company, but unseemly stresses breaking propriety through crude or improper conduct, while rude is about discourtesy and curtness toward others; they overlap without either implying the other."), Opus 3 ("Rudeness is a central form of unseemly behaviour, and unseemly is the broader breach of propriety that also covers crudeness, so they differ mainly in scope.")
  Gloss: This means behaving in ways that break social propriety, acting rudely, crudely or with bad manners in company and expecting others to tolerate it.
- **unsound** (`unsound#1`, cut-off 3) by [illogical](../../../traits/instructions/illogical.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe drawing conclusions that do not follow from the evidence; 'unsound' adds carelessness and unreliable judgment generally, while 'illogical' stresses feeling-based reasoning and clinging to broken arguments."), Opus 3 ("Both describe drawing conclusions that don't follow; illogical stresses faulty reasoning steps and clinging to broken arguments, while unsound stresses the resulting unreliable judgment, so they are near-identical concepts with differing emphasis.")
  Gloss: This means reasoning carelessly and drawing conclusions that do not follow from the evidence, so that one's judgments are often poor and cannot be relied upon.
- **unsuitable** (`unsuitable#1`, cut-off 3) by [incompetent](../../../traits/instructions/incompetent.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe falling short for a task, but 'unsuitable' is a broad mismatch (skills, temperament, or qualifications), while 'incompetent' is specifically lacking skill and botching the work; an unsuitable person may be capable but ill-fitted, and an incompetent one is unsuitable by definition of skill."), Opus 3 ("Both are about falling short of what a task demands. Unsuitable is the broader mismatch in skills, temperament or qualifications, while incompetent is specifically lacking skill and botching the work, so incompetence is roughly a narrower case of unsuitability.")
  Gloss: This means lacking the skills, temperament, or qualifications the task or role at hand demands, and falling short of what it requires.
- **utopian** (`utopian#1`, cut-off 3) by [idealistic](../../../traits/instructions/idealistic.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve aspirational visions of the best outcomes, but utopian is specifically about believing a perfect society is achievable, while idealistic is broader, covering moral principles over practicality and optimism in general."), Opus 3 ("Utopianism is idealism applied to society as a whole, the belief that a perfect social order is achievable, so it is a narrower-scope version of the same aspirational, perfection-oriented outlook.")
  Gloss: This means holding that a perfect society is achievable and steering one's views and choices toward a world in which human life is fundamentally better organized and free of its current wrongs.
- **venomous** (`venomous#1`, cut-off 3) by [hostile](../../../traits/instructions/hostile.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve antagonism toward others, but venomous is specifically spiteful, cutting speech as a habit, while hostile is broader aggressive, combative, confrontational behavior not limited to malicious remarks."), Opus 3 ("Venomous is a narrower, verbal and spiteful form of the aggressive antagonism that hostile describes more broadly.")
  Gloss: This means speaking with barbed, spiteful hostility, delivering cutting remarks and malicious jabs in one's manner as a standing habit rather than an occasional lapse.
- **verve** (`verve#1`, cut-off 3) by [manic](../../../traits/instructions/manic.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve high, restless energy and enthusiasm, but manic adds racing thoughts, pressured speech, elation and grandiosity, an extreme and unhealthy pattern that goes beyond verve's healthy liveliness."), Opus 3 ("Manic is the same high, restless enthusiasm as verve, pushed to an extreme, though it also adds grandiosity and scattered racing thoughts.")
  Gloss: This means approaching every task with bright, restless energy and visible enthusiasm, carrying a lively spirit into conversation and work alike.
- **villainous** (`villainous#1`, cut-off 4) by [evil](../../../traits/instructions/evil.json): Sonnet 3 ("Both describe a settled character of deliberate cruelty and pleasure in wrongdoing; 'evil' is slightly broader and more intense, adding corrupting others and destructive approach."), Opus 4 ("Both describe deliberate cruelty and pleasure in wrongdoing for its own sake as a settled character, so the labels are essentially interchangeable; evil's added note about drawing others into wrongdoing is minor.")
  Gloss: This means deliberately choosing cruelty and harm over decency, and taking pleasure in wrongdoing for its own sake, as a settled part of one's character.
- **virulent** (`virulent#1`, cut-off 3) by [hostile](../../../traits/instructions/hostile.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve antagonism toward others, but virulent stresses standing, bitter, poisonous contempt while hostile stresses aggressive, combative, confrontational behavior; each adds something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Virulent is hostility carried to an intense, bitter and poisonous degree, so it is the same core antagonism as hostile but stronger.")
  Gloss: This means keeping a standing attitude of intense hostility and bitterness toward others, so that one's contempt is openly poisonous rather than mild or occasional.
- **wall-flower** (`wall_flower#1`, cut-off 3) by [introverted (Big Five)](../../../traits/instructions/introverted_big_five.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve staying at the edge of social settings and being quiet, but the introverted description is broader (formal, serious, slow-paced, unmoved by thrills) while wall-flower is specifically about hanging back at gatherings."), Opus 3 ("Introversion is the broad disposition, while being a wall-flower is its narrower expression of hanging back unnoticed at gatherings, so they share a core but differ in scope.")
  Gloss: This means hanging back at the edge of social gatherings, staying quiet and unnoticed, and rarely stepping forward to talk with anyone or join in what the others are doing.
- **wallowing** (`wallowing#1`, cut-off 3) by [self-pitying](../../../traits/instructions/self_pitying.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve dwelling on one's hardships and self-pity, but wallowing stresses lingering in gloom and not recovering, while self-pitying stresses a sense of unfair treatment and seeking sympathy, so each adds something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Both are dwelling on one's own hardships in self-pity; self-pitying stresses seeking sympathy from others, while wallowing stresses lingering in the gloom.")
  Gloss: This means dwelling on one's own hardships, dredging up gloom and misery, and lingering in self-pity rather than climbing out of it.
- **wanting** (`wanting#1`, cut-off 3) by [incompetent](../../../traits/instructions/incompetent.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe falling short of what a task requires, but 'wanting' is a general deficiency in any needed quality, while 'incompetent' is specifically a lack of skill that produces botched work, so they overlap without either implying the other."), Opus 3 ("Both describe falling short of what a task requires. Wanting is broader, covering any lacking quality, character included. Incompetent is specifically a lack of skill shown in botched work, so it reads as a narrower form of being wanting.")
  Gloss: This means being deficient in some quality that the task or situation requires, so that one's work or character falls short for want of it.
- **wasting** (`wasting#1`, cut-off 3) by [inefficient](../../../traits/instructions/inefficient.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve wasting time and effort, but 'wasting' also covers resources and heedless squandering, while 'inefficient' is about poor method such as extra steps and redoing work; they share a core without either implying the other."), Opus 3 ("Inefficient is wastefulness narrowed to time and effort lost through poor process, a narrower-scope form of the broader squandering that wasting describes.")
  Gloss: This means spending one's time, effort or resources freely on things that yield little, letting them drain away without regard for their value or for what they could have achieved.
- **well-natured** (`well_natured#1`, cut-off 3) by [placid](../../../traits/instructions/placid.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve not snapping at irritations, but well-natured adds kindness and goodwill toward others while placid is about even, unruffled temperament without implying warmth."), Opus 3 ("Both centre on meeting irritations patiently rather than snapping, but well-natured adds warmth and goodwill, while placid is only the even-tempered calm.")
  Gloss: This means keeping a kind and good-tempered disposition, meeting irritations and strangers alike with patience and goodwill rather than sharpness or complaint.
- **windy** (`windy#1`, cut-off 3) by [verbose](../../../traits/instructions/verbose.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe excessive wordiness, but windy stresses bluster and empty rhetoric burying the message, while verbose stresses piling on detail and elaboration, which can be substantive."), Opus 3 ("Both mean using far more words than needed; windy adds a specific emphasis on bluster and empty rhetoric, making it a narrower variant of verbosity.")
  Gloss: This means speaking at length, padding every point with bluster and empty rhetoric until the actual message is buried under words.
- **wise-hearted** (`wise_hearted#1`, cut-off 3) by [wise](../../../traits/instructions/wise.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve sound judgment and good advice, but wise-hearted adds compassion and kindness, while wise stresses foresight and experience without requiring warmth."), Opus 3 ("Wise-hearted is wisdom in judgment and advice with an added emphasis on compassion, so it is essentially wise with a kinder slant.")
  Gloss: This means weighing matters with sound judgment and treating others with compassion, so that one's advice is both clear-sighted and kind.
- **worry-carl** (`worry_carl#1`, cut-off 3) by [neurotic (Big Five)](../../../traits/instructions/neurotic_big_five.json): Sonnet 2 ("Neuroticism includes fretting over what could go wrong, which is the target's core, but it also covers irritability, sadness, embarrassment, cravings and stress collapse, so it is a much broader trait than anticipatory worry."), Opus 3 ("Neuroticism includes the target's anticipatory fretting, but it is a much broader construct that adds irritability, sadness, self-consciousness, impulsivity and fragility under stress.")
  Gloss: This means fretting over small risks and possible mishaps, expecting things to go wrong, and voicing unease before anything has actually happened.
- **wrong-hearted** (`wrong_hearted#1`, cut-off 4) by [malevolent](../../../traits/instructions/malevolent.json): Sonnet 3 ("Both describe a settled disposition of ill will toward others, wishing and working toward their harm; the target adds taking pleasure in misfortune and spite, but the core is essentially the same."), Opus 4 ("Both describe a settled ill will that wishes and acts toward harm for others; the target adds delight in others' misfortune, but the core concept is the same.")
  Gloss: This means wishing harm on others as a settled habit, taking pleasure in their misfortune and acting on spite whenever the chance comes up.
- **yahoo** (`yahoo#1`, cut-off 3) by [unladylike](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve coarse, rough manners and speech lacking refinement, but 'unladylike' is framed around flouting genteel expectations for women and adds boisterousness and crude jokes, while 'yahoo' is general boorishness."), Opus 3 ("Both describe coarse, crude, unrefined manners. Unladylike is the same coarseness framed against genteel expectations for women, with added boisterousness.")
  Gloss: This means behaving with coarse, boorish manners and speech, showing no polish or refinement in one's dealings with other people.
- **yankee** (`yankee#1`, cut-off 3) by [American](../../../traits/instructions/american.json): Sonnet 2 ("Yankee means being from the northeastern US or New England, while American means being from the whole United States; the first is a regional subset of the second, so they share a core but differ in scope and neither is the same label."), Opus 3 ("Being a Yankee is a narrower, regional form of being from the United States, so it is the same origin concept with a smaller scope.")
  Gloss: This means being from the northeastern US or New England.

## Covered by a seed-queue entry (34 rows)

The seed queue's live entries were in the search beside the corpus traits (a word promoted from an earlier review, not yet a trait file): these rows were covered by one through the overlap walk, under the same rule as a corpus trait.  Exact-label matches with a queue entry are in the table above.

- **aiming** (`aiming#1`, cut-off 3) by [purposive](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.417292: Sonnet 3: "Both describe directing actions toward a clearly chosen goal rather than drifting, with nearly identical meaning; 'aiming' adds a bit more emphasis on not settling for less."; Opus 3: "Both describe directing actions deliberately toward chosen goals rather than drifting; aiming adds a single fixed target and a refusal to settle for less."
  Gloss: This means keeping one's sights fixed on a specific goal and steering every choice and effort toward reaching it, rather than drifting or settling for less.
- **barbarian** (`barbarian#1`, cut-off 3) by [crude](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.622819: Sonnet 3: "Both describe coarse, unrefined behavior with no regard for polish, but 'crude' focuses on vulgar language and humor while 'barbarian' is broader, covering blunt behavior and disregard for manners in general."; Opus 3: "Both describe coarse, unrefined conduct with no concern for polish. Barbarian is broader, covering all behaviour and disregard for social graces, while crude centres on vulgar language and humour."
  Gloss: This means speaking and behaving with blunt crudeness, showing no polish or refinement and no regard for the manners and cultural graces that polite society expects.
- **bawdy** (`bawdy#1`, cut-off 3) by [obscene](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.456792: Sonnet 2: "Both involve sexually explicit or suggestive speech, but bawdy is earthy, humorous and unembarrassed, while obscene is crudely lewd and offensive enough to shock, so each adds something the other lacks."; Opus 3: "Both are sexually suggestive speech. Obscene is a cruder, more offensive degree of bawdy, aimed at shocking rather than earthy humor."
  Gloss: This means speaking and joking in a sexually suggestive, earthy way, readily turning ordinary talk toward innuendo and bodily humor without embarrassment.
- **coarse-grained** (`coarse_grained#1`, cut-off 3) by [crude](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.623533: Sonnet 3: "Both describe coarse, unrefined manner lacking polish; 'crude' emphasizes vulgar language and humor while 'coarse-grained' emphasizes blunt insensitivity, so they are near-synonyms with slightly different emphasis."; Opus 3: "Both describe an unpolished, coarse manner; crude adds an emphasis on vulgar language and humor, while coarse-grained stresses blunt roughness in dealings."
  Gloss: This means speaking and behaving with blunt crudeness, showing no delicacy of manner and little polish in one's dealings with others.
- **demented** (`demented#1`, cut-off 3) by [maniacal](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.532056: Sonnet 2: "Both describe wild, irrational behaviour that has lost hold on reason, but demented stresses incoherent, illogical conduct while maniacal stresses frenzied, high-energy intensity."; Opus 3: "Both describe wild, frenzied conduct unhinged from reason; maniacal stresses frenzied intensity and loss of self-control more than incoherent lurching, a difference of emphasis."
  Gloss: This means acting wildly and irrationally, with one's words and conduct coming unhinged from sane reasoning, lurching from one frantic impulse to the next without any thread of logic.
- **dough-faced** (`dough_faced#1`, cut-off 4) by [malleable](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.385847: Sonnet 4: "Both describe yielding to the most forceful person and lacking a firm position of one's own; malleable adds plans and loyalties but is essentially the same concept."; Opus 4: "Both describe yielding to the most forceful person and having no firm position of one's own, so the two are the same concept; malleable only adds plans and loyalties, a trivial difference in scope."
  Gloss: This means yielding to whoever pushes hardest, having no firm position of one's own and bending one's views to match the nearest strong opinion.
- **drifting** (`drifting#1`, cut-off 3) by [aimless](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.599374: Sonnet 4: "Both describe going through life without goals or direction and letting circumstances decide; 'aimless' adds only slightly more emphasis on non-commitment, so they are nearly interchangeable."
  Gloss: This means going through each day without a goal, letting circumstances decide where one lands next, and feeling no pull toward any particular purpose or future.
- **flap-mouthed** (`flap_mouthed#1`, cut-off 3) by [chattering](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.5313: Sonnet 2: "Both describe constant, non-stop talking, but flap-mouthed adds indiscriminate disclosure of gossip without filter and not listening, while chattering emphasizes a quick, easy stream of small talk."; Opus 3: "Both mean incessant talking that fills every silence; flap-mouthed goes further, adding unfiltered, indiscreet spilling of gossip and a failure to listen."
  Gloss: This means talking constantly and indiscriminately, letting every thought and piece of gossip spill out without filter, so that one rarely stops to listen.
- **flimsy** (`flimsy#1`, cut-off 4) by [malleable](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.405983: Sonnet 3: "Both describe dropping opinions and yielding to pressure from others for lack of firm conviction, so they are essentially the same concept, with malleable adding loyalties and plans."; Opus 4: "Both describe yielding to pressure and abandoning one's positions for lack of firm conviction; malleable adds plans and loyalties but the concept is essentially identical."
  Gloss: This means giving way to whatever pressure or opinion is nearest, dropping one's stated positions when challenged, and lacking the firmness of conviction that would hold one's views in place.
- **floating** (`floating#1`, cut-off 3) by [aimless](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.517769: Sonnet 3: "Both describe drifting without fixed purpose or commitment; 'floating' adds not staying in one place and moving between undertakings, while 'aimless' stresses the lack of goals, but the core is essentially the same."; Opus 3: "Both describe drifting without fixed purpose or commitment; floating adds moving between undertakings and places, a slight emphasis difference."
  Gloss: This means drifting from one undertaking to the next without settling on a fixed purpose, staying in no one place and holding no firm commitment to where one's efforts or life are headed.
- **gross** (`gross#1`, cut-off 3) by [crude](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.592857: Sonnet 2: "Both involve coarse, vulgar behavior, but 'gross' also covers repulsive manners and disregard for others' comfort, while 'crude' centers on vulgar speech and humor, so each adds something the other lacks."; Opus 3: "Both describe coarse, vulgar behaviour. Gross is the broader, stronger version, adding coarse table manners and offensiveness that makes people recoil, while crude centres on rough speech and humour."
  Gloss: This means behaving in a crude, offensive way, with coarse table manners, lewd remarks and a disregard for other people's comfort that leaves them recoiling.
- **hare-mad** (`hare_mad#1`, cut-off 3) by [maniacal](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.457464: Sonnet 2: "Both describe wild, frenzied behavior as if reason has slipped, but hare-mad stresses fast, jittery, restless excitability while maniacal stresses intense, unhinged, possibly menacing loss of control."; Opus 3: "Both describe wild, frenzied energy that undermines clear thinking; maniacal is the same quality pushed further, to a total loss of reason and control."
  Gloss: This means carrying oneself with a wild, frenzied energy, speaking and moving in fast, excitable bursts as though hardly able to keep still or think straight.
- **ill-tongued** (`ill_tongued#1`, cut-off 3) by [insulting](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.43373: Sonnet 3: "Both involve habitually using cutting, hurtful words toward others; ill-tongued emphasizes harsh, spiteful speech while insulting emphasizes demeaning and belittling, so they are nearly the same with a slight difference in emphasis."; Opus 3: "Both describe habitually using cutting, hurtful speech toward others; insulting stresses belittling and mocking specifically, while ill-tongued covers harsh, spiteful speech more broadly."
  Gloss: This means speaking harshly and spitefully to others, using cutting words and barbs even when a gentler remark would serve just as well.
- **invasive** (`invasive#1`, cut-off 3) by [meddling](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.624571: Sonnet 3: "Both describe prying into others' private business and inserting oneself into matters that aren't one's own; invasive adds physical/space crowding while meddling emphasizes interfering in decisions, a minor difference in emphasis."; Opus 3: "Both describe prying into others' private affairs and getting involved uninvited; invasive also stresses crowding personal space, while meddling stresses interfering in decisions, a difference only of emphasis."

- **jack nasty** (`jack_nasty#1`, cut-off 3) by [insulting](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.43559: Sonnet 3: "Both involve habitually offending people through cutting speech, but 'jack nasty' is broader mean-spirited unpleasantness while 'insulting' is specifically demeaning and belittling remarks."; Opus 3: "Both describe habitually offending people with insulting speech; jack nasty is a broader mean-spirited unpleasantness, while insulting narrows it to belittling remarks."
  Gloss: This means being mean-spirited and unpleasant to others, speaking in ways that offend and insult people as a matter of course.
- **jack-a-lent** (`jack_a_lent#1`, cut-off 3) by [pathetic](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.429675: Sonnet 2: "Both describe feebleness and inadequacy, but jack-a-lent stresses ineffectuality and a lack of influence, while pathetic stresses contemptible inadequacy of character; they share a core and each adds something the other lacks."; Opus 3: "Both describe a person who is feeble and ineffectual.   Gloss: This means being feeble and ineffectual, with one's efforts achieving nothing and one's word carrying no force with anyone.
- **lacking** (`lacking#1`, cut-off 3) by [pathetic](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.297638: Sonnet 2: "Both describe a persona who falls short in character, but 'pathetic' adds feebleness and contempt-inducing inadequacy, while 'lacking' is about missing a specific essential quality and failing to hold firm; they overlap without either implying the other."; Opus 3: "Both describe a deficiency of character that leads to failure; pathetic adds a general feebleness and the contempt it earns, so it is the same shortfall carried further with a different emphasis."

- **leech** (`leech#1`, cut-off 4) by [parasitic](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.461445: Sonnet 4: "Both describe living off others' resources and effort while giving nothing back, so they are essentially the same concept with only slightly different wording."; Opus 4: "Both describe living off others' resources and effort while giving nothing back, so the concepts are the same and either label could replace the other."

- **long-tongued** (`long_tongued#1`, cut-off 3) by [chattering](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.525765: Sonnet 3: "Both describe talking at length and filling silences; chattering adds a quick, light stream of small remarks, while long-tongued stresses prolonging conversations past others' interest, so they are nearly the same with a slight difference in emphasis."; Opus 3: "Both describe incessant talking that fills silences, with chattering adding a quick, light, small-remark flavour and long-tongued stressing length and dominating conversation, so they differ only in emphasis."
  Gloss: This means talking freely and at length, filling most silences with one's own words and keeping conversations going well past the point where others have stopped.
- **masking** (`masking#1`, cut-off 3) by [repressed](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.48941: Sonnet 2: "Both involve keeping feelings from showing, but masking is a composed outward concealment while repressed is an inward holding-in or suppression of the feelings; each adds something the other lacks."; Opus 3: "Both describe keeping one's feelings hidden from others; repressed stresses the strain of holding them in, while masking stresses the composed outward facade."

- **mock** (`mock#1`, cut-off 3) by [insulting](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.4431: Sonnet 3: "Both describe habitually ridiculing and belittling others with cutting remarks; 'insulting' is slightly broader (demeaning in general) while 'mock' stresses scornful humor, but the core is nearly identical."; Opus 3: "Both describe belittling others with scornful remarks; mocking stresses ridicule and jokes, while insulting is the broader habit of demeaning put-downs."

- **neat-handed** (`neat_handed#1`, cut-off 3) by [deft](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.625864: Sonnet 4: "Both describe quick, precise, skilled handling of tasks with the hands, so the two labels are nearly interchangeable."

- **nimble-fingered** (`nimble_fingered#1`, cut-off 3) by [deft](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.461733: Sonnet 3: "Both describe quick, precise, skilled handwork; 'deft' is slightly broader (can apply to skilled handling generally) but essentially the same concept."; Opus 3: "Both describe quick, precise, skilled handwork; nimble-fingered narrows it to fine finger work with small or delicate items, while deft covers skilled handling more generally."

- **nosey** (`nosey#1`, cut-off 3) by [nosy](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.644528: Sonnet 4: "'Nosey' and 'nosy' are two spellings of the same word, and both descriptions cover prying into others' private affairs, finances and relationships."

- **pent** (`pent#1`, cut-off 3) by [repressed](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.558972: Sonnet 3: "Both describe keeping strong feelings bottled up and not letting them show, with only slight differences in wording and emphasis."; Opus 4: "Both describe holding strong feelings in and refusing to let them show; the descriptions are essentially interchangeable."

- **pointless** (`pointless#1`, cut-off 3) by [aimless](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.626347: Sonnet 3: "Both describe drifting without a goal, but 'pointless' stresses efforts coming to nothing and nothing being finished, while 'aimless' stresses lack of direction and commitment, so each adds something the other lacks."; Opus 3: "Both describe drifting without a goal; pointless adds an emphasis on efforts coming to nothing and never being finished, a difference of emphasis rather than of concept."

- **prying** (`prying#1`, cut-off 3) by [nosy](../../../seed_queue.json) (seed queue, candidate), cosine 0.578373: Sonnet 4: "Both describe intrusively probing into others' private affairs with a sense of entitlement, so either label could replace the other."

## Both ends similar (orthogonal to the pair?) (6 rows)

Decision 13 (Roger's rule): the relation call marked both members of a recorded pair (every corner of a triangle or simplex) similar, so the candidate is probably orthogonal to the pair's axis: neither member was judged or may cover it.  Cosines to the candidate; overlap readings where any are on record.

- **attracted** (`attracted#1`, covered by [lustful](../../../traits/instructions/lustful.json), cut-off 3).  Gloss: This means being romantically or sexually drawn to others.
  - pair: [gay](../../../traits/instructions/gay.json) (cosine 0.485); [straight](../../../traits/instructions/straight.json) (cosine 0.494)
- **opulent** (`opulent#1`, covered by [wealthy](../../../traits/instructions/wealthy.json), cut-off 3).  Gloss: This means living in wealth and luxury, with abundance and costly possessions filling everyday life as a matter of course.
  - pair: [new money](../../../traits/instructions/new_money.json) (cosine 0.359); [old money](../../../traits/instructions/old_money.json) (cosine 0.316)
- **swift** (`swift#1`, covered by [hurried](../../../traits/instructions/hurried.json), cut-off 3).  Gloss: This means acting the moment a need appears, answering messages and requests without delay, and moving from decision to action with no pause for second thoughts.
  - pair: [proactive](../../../traits/instructions/proactive.json) (cosine 0.280); [reactive](../../../traits/instructions/reactive.json) (cosine 0.342)
- **directing** (`directing#1`, new, cut-off 3).  Gloss: This means taking charge of a group's work or a production, setting its direction, assigning roles, and making the calls that keep others moving toward a shared end.
  - pair: [transactional](../../../traits/instructions/transactional.json) (cosine 0.241); [transformational](../../../traits/instructions/transformational.json) (cosine 0.282)
- **genius** (`genius#1`, new, cut-off 3).  Gloss: This means grasping difficult ideas faster and more deeply than most people, and bringing that unusual intellectual ability to every problem one is given.
  - pair: [analytical](../../../traits/instructions/analytical.json) (cosine 0.101); [intuitive](../../../traits/instructions/intuitive.json) (cosine 0.292)
  - pair: [generalist](../../../traits/instructions/generalist.json) (cosine 0.239); [specialist](../../../traits/instructions/specialist.json) (cosine 0.213)
- **instilling** (`instilling#1`, new, cut-off 3).  Gloss: This means patiently teaching values or ideas to others over time, one lesson and one example at a time, until they take them on as their own.
  - pair: [didactic](../../../traits/instructions/didactic.json) (cosine 0.312); [socratic](../../../traits/instructions/socratic.json) (cosine 0.173)
